Start here

What the system does and what exists now

Current source and v6 baseline, checked 12 October 2026; recorded verification from 9 October

Heimdal sends measurements from gateway devices identified by clsbox. The Stats App keeps those measurements for a configurable retention period and makes them available to authorised tenant readers. Five stat types are recognised: lorastats for radio activity, ltestats for cellular activity, sysstats for system health, omsstats for OMS activity and netstats for network interfaces.

AreaImplemented baselineRemaining qualification
InputTwo POST paths, envelope/skew/limit gates, byte-preserving confirmed publication and JSON acknowledgementInbound identity and external tenant binding remain open
WriterAll five consumers; payload validation, qualified batch writers, V8/V9 LTE/network tables, idempotency, retry/DLQ and retentionLTE masking/representation approval D-12 P3 still gates merge/shared release
APIFour GET route shapes for all five types; tenant isolation, signed cursors and wire reconstructionD-09 confirmation, independent review and production performance remain due
OperationsAutomatic Prometheus/exporter startup; four successful scrapes; alerts, dashboards and local drillsProduction capacity, backups/recovery and live cleanup evidence remain operational work
Deliveryv6 offline bundle, ten images, eight default services, optional Grafana and a shipped 40-request collection; current source collection has 46 requestsLocal evaluation configuration; upstream feed confirmation Q15g remains open

Design sizing assumes 160,000 gateways reporting every ten minutes: about 267 records per second per type, 1,333 across five types and 115.2 million records per day. At 90 days, that is approximately 10.4 billion rows across all types. These figures explain the partitioning, retention and scaling choices; they are design inputs, not a measurement of the current local bundle.

How to read this guide

The numbered phases follow the project plan. Work overlapped: operational drills ran before the final API implementation, and a foundation decision may have been enforced in a later September change. Each box appears where its rule is used; its linked record preserves the actual date. Read the explanation first and expand full records when you need implementation or approval detail.

The decision

Acceptance is a durable handoff

Decision. Return HTTP success only after a routed publish is confirmed, and acknowledge a consumed message only after database commit.

Why. Heimdal treats successful delivery as permission to move on; a premature success can remove the upstream recovery copy.

Effect. Broker acceptance and database persistence remain distinct. Retention and the signed late arrival policy still apply; acceptance does not promise storage forever.

DL-033 · DL-118 · DL-122 · DL-133

Phase zero

Reconcile requirements and unblock the build

The authority and coordination decisions that preceded implementation

0.1 Establish one authoritative baseline

Use v1.2 amendments in docs/requirements/ for normative requirements; repository ADR 001–007 for architectural decisions; docs/schemas/ for validation; docs/contracts/openapi.yaml and openapi-input.yaml for read and ingest contracts; docs/decisions/DECISIONS.md for reasons and dated changes; and docs/architecture/FLOW.md for delivery and failure paths. ADRs outrank the running decision log on architectural conflicts.

Historical v1.0 sources preserve provenance but are superseded where amendments changed their database, envelope or converter assumptions. The ARD's own ADR IDs refer to different subjects and are cited as ARD-ADR-nn. Requirement IDs, decision question IDs, gate IDs and DL IDs are separate namespaces. The tracker and old planning notes explain earlier sequencing; current code and later ratified decisions determine the implemented baseline.

The decision

Reconcile the requirements before coding

Decision. Use the v1.2 amendments and repository ADRs; retain the original documents as historical provenance.

Why. The older material disagreed about MongoDB, the envelope and OMS timestamp conversion.

Effect. Requirements and decisions are traceable; ARD decision identifiers use a separate namespace.

The baseline and coordination decisions

Expand a dated record for the complete rationale, alternatives, consequences and verification. These records belong to this phase. Later amendments remain visible in their historical context.

DL-001 — Working documents live in claude/; the repository root holds a pointer only

Claude Code auto-loads the root CLAUDE.md only. Keeping the content there would mean the project context and the twelve other working documents live in two different places and drift. The pointer gives auto-loading *and* one directory.

16 Aug 2026 · RATIFIED · claude/CLAUDE.md §10, README.md

Decision. Every working document lives in claude/. The root CLAUDE.md contains a single @claude/CLAUDE.md import and nothing else.

Why. Claude Code auto-loads the root CLAUDE.md only. Keeping the content there would mean the project context and the twelve other working documents live in two different places and drift. The pointer gives auto-loading *and* one directory.

Rejected. Content in the root CLAUDE.md — splits the working set across two locations. A symlink — invisible in a diff, and does not survive every checkout cleanly.

Consequence. Edit claude/CLAUDE.md; never the root file except to change the import path.

DL-002 — Requirement and ADR IDs are cited in code, commits, and configuration

This project's hardest bugs are invisible ones: filtering on maintenant, a TTL on the DLQ, timestamps read as milliseconds. Every one of them looks like reasonable code. The citation is what tells the next reader that a line is load-bearing before they simplify it away.

16 Aug 2026 · RATIFIED · IMPLEMENTATION_RULES.md §11

Decision. Behaviour driven by a requirement names its STA-nn and the governing ADR — in the commit message, in a comment on the class or method, and in application.properties next to the property.

Why. This project's hardest bugs are invisible ones: filtering on maintenant, a TTL on the DLQ, timestamps read as milliseconds. Every one of them looks like reasonable code. The citation is what tells the next reader that a line is load-bearing before they simplify it away.

Rejected. A separate traceability matrix — it is maintained by someone remembering to, which is to say it is not maintained.

Consequence. A change with no requirement basis is not "quick", it is undecided. That is the point.

DL-003 — The ARD's ADRs are cited as ARD-ADR-nn

The ARD's ADR-004 is at-least-once delivery. Ours is OMS timestamp normalisation. Two documents, one label, opposite subjects — someone was going to implement the wrong one.

18 Aug 2026 · RATIFIED · Gate G5

Decision. Repository ADRs keep the bare ADR-nnn form and stay authoritative. Datahub_Core_StatsApp_ARD_v1_1.pdf carries its own ADR-001…010, which mean different things; cite those as ARD-ADR-nn.

Why. The ARD's ADR-004 is at-least-once delivery. Ours is OMS timestamp normalisation. Two documents, one label, opposite subjects — someone was going to implement the wrong one.

Rejected. Renumbering the repository ADRs — they are cited from the SRS amendment, the OpenAPI contract, and the code.

Consequence. An unqualified "ADR-004" always means this repository.

DL-004 — DECISIONS.md and FLOW.md are added as working documents

The reasoning behind this project is real and unusually good, but it is spread across six ADRs, two gate tables, a stop/go register, a 23,000-word open questions status, and a lot of exceptionally load-bearing code comments. There was no single list of *what was decided and why*, and no single description of *how a record actually travels through the system*. Both are the first things a new contributor — or a returning one — needs, and both were reconstructable only by reading everything.

29 Aug 2026 · ENGINEERING

Decision. Add this file and claude/FLOW.md to claude/, in the existing uppercase naming convention, and index both in README.md and claude/CLAUDE.md §10.

Why. The reasoning behind this project is real and unusually good, but it is spread across six ADRs, two gate tables, a stop/go register, a 23,000-word open questions status, and a lot of exceptionally load-bearing code comments. There was no single list of *what was decided and why*, and no single description of *how a record actually travels through the system*. Both are the first things a new contributor — or a returning one — needs, and both were reconstructable only by reading everything.

Rejected. Lowercase decisions.md / flow.md as literally requested — claude/ is uniformly uppercase (CLAUDE.md, SKILLS.md, DEVELOPMENT.md); one lowercase pair would look like a stray file. Putting the ledger in docs/decisions/ — that directory holds *dated decision records* from specific sittings, and this is a continuous index across all of them.

Consequence. Two more documents to keep current. DL-002's rule now covers them: a decision is not implemented until it is logged.

DL-005 — Changes are quizzed before they are made

On this project the expensive failures are silent: a query on maintenant passes every test written against the supplied samples; a timestamp read as milliseconds writes 1970 without an error; a TTL on the DLQ deletes the last copy of a record and reports success. Code review does not catch these, because the code looks correct — only someone who *knows the trap* catches them. The quiz makes the reviewer's understanding a precondition of the change rather than a hope.

29 Aug 2026 · ENGINEERING · lands in IMPLEMENTATION_RULES.md §9

Decision. Before implementing a change, Claude quizzes the requester on the decisions that change depends on. A wrong or unsure answer is not a failure to move past — it is answered with an explanation, and then the quiz is asked again. Implementation starts after the quiz is passed.

Why. On this project the expensive failures are silent: a query on maintenant passes every test written against the supplied samples; a timestamp read as milliseconds writes 1970 without an error; a TTL on the DLQ deletes the last copy of a record and reports success. Code review does not catch these, because the code looks correct — only someone who *knows the trap* catches them. The quiz makes the reviewer's understanding a precondition of the change rather than a hope.

There is a second reason. The requester is the approver on the 18 and 29 August decision sets. An approver who cannot restate the reasoning is signing, not deciding.

Rejected. Explaining the change without checking comprehension — the failure mode being guarded against is exactly a confident, wrong mental model, and an explanation cannot detect one. Quizzing only on request — it would be skipped precisely on the days it matters.

Consequence. Changes take longer to start. That is the trade being bought deliberately: the traps in CLAUDE.md §6 each cost far more than a quiz.

DL-114 — D-10 and D-12 lose the halves that were engineering's all along

4 Sep 2026 · ENGINEERING · D-10, D-12, ADR-003 D8, DL-099, DL-102, DL-103 · T-065, T-066, T-034

Decision. Four fixes, taken together because they share one shape: each was an item sitting on a *someone else* row that nobody else could have done.

1. The Writer refuses a deployment it cannot honour (T-065). stats.writer.stat-type defaults to all, ADR-003 D8 requires exactly one type per deployment in production, and nothing refused it. StatTypeDeploymentGuard now fails the context under the prod profile, and refuses an unrecognised value in *every* profile. The second half is the dangerous one: a typo makes every per-type Condition evaluate false, so the Writer starts, binds no listener, consumes nothing and still reports ready — readiness has no opinion about whether a listener exists. all outside prod stays legal and logs a WARN; a guard that forbade it everywhere would simply get the property deleted.

2. The Dockerfile copies every module (D-10 residual). It listed the four original modules and never stats-testsupport, a module since 3 Sep (DL-099). It built green only because bootJar -x test never resolves the test classpath and the warm-up layer ends in || true. Both COPY lines added; the || true stays, now with a comment saying what it cannot catch. settings.gradle.kts also still called itself "Four modules" above a list of five.

3. P1, P2 and P3 are adopted (D-12). The inventory offered five proposals and split them by reversibility. The three that need no signature are taken: P1 never log an ltestats or netstats payload at any level including DEBUG; P2 log the exception type and message, never PostgreSQL's DETAIL: line; P3 T-034 does not merge until the masking questions are answered. P1 and P2 are now in DEVELOPMENT.md §7, which is the authoritative logging rule; P3 is T-034's Blocked by cell rather than a line in a table nobody schedules from. P4 and P5 remain security's — they are irreversible, and adopting the reversible three pre-decides neither.

Why adopt logging rules for code that does not exist. stats-writer has no ERROR or WARN path at all until T-024 and T-057. That is the argument for writing them now, not against it: a logging rule adopted after the log line exists is a rule someone has to go back and enforce, and the first ERROR path in this system will be written while chasing a failure, which is the worst moment to be deciding what may be printed. Same reasoning as DL-113.

4. Both rows had no task, and that is why they sat. D-10's STAT_TYPE gap and the whole of D-12 had no T-nnn, so nothing in the Tasks sheet surfaced either — the defect the 29 August pass caught for Q14a, repeating twice. T-065 (done) and T-066 (pending T-024 and T-057) now exist.

Consequence. D-10 is platform-only: runtime platform, image registry, promotion path, resource requests and limits. Its resource-sizing item is no longer blocked on Q13b either, which DL-104 answered — though DL-104 defers sizing down until T-050's histogram confirms the jitter. D-12 keeps five questions for security plus two items re-owned to platform, and engineering's remaining obligation is to honour what it just adopted.

DL-116 — The 4 September reconciliation is finished in the files that are not Markdown

Commit 874ef77 reconciled claude/*.md and the ADRs and stopped there, so the correction landed everywhere except the files an engineer actually opens while working — a live properties file, the normative schema catalogue, and the API contract. A decision that is right in the decision log and wrong in the config is worse than one that is merely undocumented: the config reads as current and gets believed.

5 Sep 2026 · HOUSEKEEPING · DL-105, DL-106, DL-108, DL-112 · T-060, T-063, T-064, T-026

Decision. Seven artifacts still stated the reversed ADR-007 §4 as current policy, eight working days after DL-105 reversed it. All are now corrected: stats-writer/.../application.properties, .env.example, docs/schemas/lorastats-v1.schema.json, docs/schemas/SCHEMA_CATALOGUE.md, docs/schemas/fixtures/MANIFEST.md, valid/lorastats-production-canonical.json, and docs/contracts/openapi.yaml. envelope-v1.schema.json additionally still carried Q15b as an open question (DL-106 answered it) and described status as never interpreted (DL-108 meters it).

stats.writer.dlq.alert-depth.lorastats and STATS_DLQ_ALERT_DEPTH_LORASTATS are deleted. Both existed only to hold a threshold for a permanently non-empty lorastats DLQ, which DL-105 removes. A uniform alert-depth.default=0 is correct again.

Why. Commit 874ef77 reconciled claude/*.md and the ADRs and stopped there, so the correction landed everywhere except the files an engineer actually opens while working — a live properties file, the normative schema catalogue, and the API contract. A decision that is right in the decision log and wrong in the config is worse than one that is merely undocumented: the config reads as current and gets believed.

The distinction every edit preserves. DL-105 changed the decision; T-063 changes the behaviour, and it is not built. Today only the bare/integer dialect validates and the other two still dead-letter. [Superseded 8 September 2026 by DL-127: T-063 landed and all three now validate. Every passage this entry corrected was corrected again that day — which is the cost of stating a build state in a document, and still cheaper than stating a decision the code does not implement.] Every corrected passage says so explicitly rather than describing the end state as though it had arrived — the failure mode in the other direction, and the one DL-100 was written about. The two invalid/lorastats-dialect-*.json fixtures already had this exactly right; the wording follows them.

Consequence. A grep for dead-letter by design, most urgent question for ZC, or BLOCKED on D5b returns nothing outside the dated historical documents (claude/OUTBOX_03SEP2026.md, the ADR narratives that record *why* Q15a was escalated), where the past tense is correct and must stay.

DL-125 — The tracker gets a sequence, because a backlog does not answer "what next"

5 Sep 2026 · HOUSEKEEPING · DL-113…DL-124, DL-095, DL-105, D-12 P3 · T-035, T-037, T-047, T-060

Decision. Five changes, all recorded in Stats_App_Tracker.xlsx and reflected in the working documents.

1. A Next Up sheet. Fourteen ranked rows across both owners, each carrying the gate that must close before it starts. The Tasks sheet stays the backlog; this sheet is the order. claude/CLAUDE.md §7, PROJECT_STATUS_AND_PLAN.md §3 and README.md all point at it rather than restating it. 2. The fan-out starts at sysstats, not ltestats. T-034 is blocked on D-12 P3 — whether imsi is stored masked or raw and whether it may be a query predicate — and T-035 is blocked on nothing. Its only sharp edge is timespan arriving as the string "300", which is the T-062 lesson learned on the cheapest type. 3. T-037 (netstats) now depends on T-033, not T-034. The old dependency made netstats inherit a block about a field it does not have. netstats is last because it is the jsonb hybrid the typed-column reference slice templates least well — a plan, not a prerequisite. 4. The six missing decisions and four missing evidence rows were added — DL-113, DL-114, DL-115, DL-116, DL-117 and DL-124 on the Decisions sheet, DN-41…DN-44 on the Done sheet. The tracker had recorded the ingest work of 5 September and none of the six decisions taken the same day. 5. The fixture figures are corrected to 76 JSON fixtures and 88 assertions. *(79 and 91 since T-063 and T-064 on 8 September — DL-127, DL-128.)* The sheet said 77 and 87; validate-fixtures.py says 88 over 76, and valid/ gained lorastats-clsbox-lowercase.json with DL-115. The stale A1:H22 range on the Open Questions table — spanning fifteen deleted rows — was shrunk to the six that remain.

Why a sheet rather than a paragraph. The Tasks sheet answers *what is outstanding* and answers it well: sixty-six rows with acceptance criteria, requirement IDs and blockers. It does not answer *what do I open now*, and neither does ID order — T-034 precedes T-035 and must not be started first, T-060 precedes nothing and must wait for T-063. Every reader was re-deriving that sequence from dependencies, priority and three "blocked by" columns, and two of them derived it differently.

Why the ordering corrections are decisions and not tidying. A false dependency and a true one are indistinguishable to whoever reads the row next. T-037 → T-034 would have held netstats behind a data-classification question about IMSI for as long as D-12 P3 stayed open, and the reason would have been invisible: the sheet would simply have shown netstats as not startable. That is the same failure as a stale OPEN item — the expensive kind, because someone eventually spends a day re-deciding it (DL-116's opening paragraph makes the same point about a live properties file).

What this deliberately does not do. It does not change a single status. Nothing was marked DONE that was not already DONE in the repository, and nothing was reprioritised except by naming the gate that was already true. The one behavioural claim it makes — that the fan-out may start at sysstats — follows from D-12 P3 blocking ltestats alone, which DL-114 established on 4 September.

DL-139 — The Datahub API guide is reconciled against this project: one glossary correction, four questions raised, nothing reopened

12 Sep 2026 · ENGINEERING · ADR-006, ADR-007, DL-106, DL-108, Q8a-2 · T-034, T-038, T-050

What was read. ZENNER_Datahub_API_Guide v4.0 (11 Sep 2026, Naman Vashishtha) — the consolidated reference for the platform the Stats App plugs into, cross-checked against the Datahub OpenAPI spec and live instances. It is kept outside this repository (~/Desktop/work/Naman/Work/Documentation/APIs/) because it describes ZC's platform, not ours. Every field name it shows for lorastats and ltestats matches the schemas in docs/schemas/; the envelope's ts object already accepts the extra members the platform carries on measurement documents (lns, parser, lnsnano). No schema, column, or contract changes. What follows is what did change, and what is now a question.

Decision 1 — maintenant moves with tenant and equals it, and the trap is sharper for that, not softer. Guide §1.3 describes maintenant as "the tenant that originally owned" an object, and a first reading of this entry took that to mean the two fields differ after a tenant move. That reading was wrong and was corrected the same day by Naman from live behaviour: when a device is moved to another tenant with the tenant-change call, *both* tenant and maintenant are set to the new tenant name. They are equal — on the 29 Aug production sample, on the /statistics example in guide §7.6 (tenant: brunata, maintenant: brunata), and on every record this system will receive. claude/CLAUDE.md §4 had called it "the *managing* tenant"; it now says what it is: the maintaining tenant, set to the same value as tenant and moved with it.

The isolation rule does not move an inch — tenant is the only isolation field (ADR-006). What changes is the *reason* the trap is the highest-risk item in the project: since the two values are equal on every real record, no production data can ever reveal a filter on the wrong field. A query on maintenant returns the right rows today, tomorrow, and on the day the platform changes what maintenant means. The only thing that can catch it is a fixture in which the two values were made to differ on purpose — which is why valid/<type>-tenant-isolation-pair.json exists and why that rule in §6 is not a suggestion.

> Corrected 18 September 2026 (DL-146). "Equal on every record this system will > receive" was true of every tenant but one. On the minol tenant, roughly one live > record in ten carries tenant: minol-gateway with maintenant: minol (Naman, from > live data). The isolation rule and the fixture rule are unchanged; what changes is > the wording of the trap — see DL-146 decision 1.

Decision 2 — four questions are recorded, and none is answered here. Each was derived from the guide, not from ZC, and the guide's §9 documents *sensor* forwarding — whether gateway statistics use the same path is not stated. So they are questions.

Ref Question Why it matters Owner
Q8a-3 Is the stats push to Input a Heimdal ForwardRule? Guide §9: a ForwardRule is {url, type, id, maxRetry}, transports `AMQP \ HTTP \ AMQP+ELEMENT \ MSSQL \ OCHP \ MQTT`, and *"credentials go in the URL"* Two of our assumptions rest on the answer. Auth: credentials-in-URL is HTTP basic, which `stats.input.auth.mode=none\mtls\oauth2 (DL-120) cannot express — Q8a-2's "OAuth2 unless you object" may be proposing a mode Heimdal does not have. Retry: rule 8 says Heimdal retries every non-2xx; maxRetry` says it retries a *bounded* number of times, so a sustained 503 burns a budget and the loss is on their side, invisible to us ZC (with Q8a-2)
D16 A gateway that changes tenant keeps its pre-move rows under the *old* tenant. The new tenant's token cannot see them (ADR-006 puts tenant in every query). Is that the intended reading? Ours to decide. Recommended position: yes — a row's tenant is the tenant at measurement time, exactly as Heimdal stamped it (ADR-007: no rename layer, no rewriting). The alternative, re-labelling history on a move, would require the Writer to read the inventory service, which is a dependency this project does not have and should not acquire. Record as an ADR-006 amendment when decided; until then it is a support question waiting to happen. Ratified 17 Sep 2026 — DL-142; ADR-006 amended Engineering
— status vocabulary: measurement documents carry ["ok"] (guide §1.8); stats records carry ["conversion ok"] Adds one known-good value to the DL-108 residual. If a stats record ever carries ["ok"], records_upstream_status_not_ok{type} lights on ordinary traffic. The counter will show it; T-050's alert threshold should not be zero until the first 24 h of ingest have enumerated the real values Engineering (T-050)
— clsbox = FFFFFFFFFFFF means *synthetic / no gateway* (guide §1.8) Nothing to build: the CHECK (clsbox ~ '^[0-9A-F]{12}$') accepts it and it is a valid device identifier as far as this system is concerned. Recorded so an operator who sees it in a WARN does not open an incident —

Two things the guide confirms rather than changes. The platform already serves sysstats/lorastats/ltestats through POST /datastore/aggregate/pageable with $match {clsbox, type, ts.device range} and $sort ts.device (guide §7.3) — the same query the (tenant, clsbox, ts_device DESC, uid DESC) index was designed for, and a parity oracle for T-041/T-043: the same gateway and window fetched from both sides must agree. And /statistics/statisticevents (guide §7.6) uses the field name gateway and milliseconds — which is almost certainly where the SRS's wrong vocabulary came from (ADR-007 §1, trap "the SRS field names are not the wire field names"). Consistency across documents was never evidence; here is the document they were consistent with.

One data point for D-12, not an answer. The guide's ltestats example (§7.3) returns imsi, lac and cid in the clear to any holder of the datastore.read scope. That is the platform's current posture, and D-12 P3 is the question of whether ours should match it. It is recorded here so the D-12 conversation starts from what the platform does today rather than from a blank page.

DL-152 — The documents and the tracker are reconciled to DL-145…DL-151, and one sentence of two ADR-006 amendments is settled

21 September 2026 · ENGINEERING · DL-002, DL-125 (the previous reconciliation), DL-145…DL-151 · approver: Naman Vashishtha

Trigger. Stats_App_Tracker.xlsx had last been touched on 19 September and had never received the 18 September work at all: no Decisions row for DL-145 or DL-146, no Open Questions rows for Q15e–Q15g, no Done rows for the inbound OpenAPI twin or the live-data reconciliation. DL-149…DL-151 of 20–21 September were in CLAUDE.md, FLOW.md, DEVELOPMENT.md and the contracts, and nowhere else: README.md still said *Status (12 September)*, FLOW.md's status table was dated 12 September and listed nothing merged since, PROJECT_STATUS_AND_PLAN.md had no "what next" after 12 September, and CLAUDE.md §9, IMPLEMENTATION_RULES.md §9 and README.md all said *nine of the eleven* loss windows fail silently while FLOW.md §8 has had fifteen since DL-149. DL-151's own residual said, in as many words, that the tracker needed a row.

Decision. Reconcile everything in one change, as DL-125 did on 5 September:

1. Tracker. Decisions rows DL-145, DL-146, DL-149, DL-150, DL-151, DL-152; ADR-006 and ADR-007 status cells updated. Tasks: T-067 (the device-scoped read paths, Phase 4, gated on D-09), T-068 (per-door authentication and *token tenant = record tenant* on the external door — BLOCKED on Q8a-2, the first BLOCKED row since 4 September, and BLOCKED rather than TODO because the mechanism to build against does not exist yet), T-069 (docker/demo.sh, DONE 20 Sep); notes on T-005, T-012, T-013, T-017, T-018, T-027, T-036 and T-040…T-046; T-040's acceptance text loses the clsbox query parameter. Done: DN-62…DN-68. Open Questions: Q15e, Q15f, Q15g added; Q8a-2's *Blocks* now names the external door; Q8a-3 narrowed by DL-145; D-09's text says the contract being confirmed changed. Traps 31–34 (ahead-only pre-creation, an MDC key repeated on a log line, a door that authenticates the caller and binds nothing to the tenant, "from to now" as to = now), coverage header re-dated. Notes: five rows, 18–21 Sep. Next Up re-ranked: the two "send to ZC / Hasan" rows rise because each now gates a task (T-068, T-037); T-068 sits last as BLOCKED so nobody exposes the external door by accident. Every table's ref re-extended to its last row — four had drifted, which is why filters were missing rows. 2. Documents. README.md status re-dated 21 Sep with the 20–21 Sep work, the Q13b paragraph corrected (it still called Q13b *the most valuable open question* seventeen days after DL-104 answered it), fixture and trap counts refreshed, the tracker's location stated correctly (one level above the checkout, not committed — README.md said *repository root*). FLOW.md: status table re-dated with eleven rows for what merged since 12 Sep; §4.4's pre-create line no longer says *ahead*. CLAUDE.md 1.18: three §6 traps, the D16 row, the ADR-006 status row, §9's count, §10's tracker row. PROJECT_STATUS_AND_PLAN.md: a 21 Sep "what next" table, Q8a-3 in §2b-2, the samples row (it said 79 files / 91 assertions — the numbers of 8 Sep), Phase 4 and 5 text. CODE_START_CHECKLIST.md: D-03 names both doors, D-09 says the contract changed, Phase 4 and 5 state. SKILLS.md 1.5: Skill 1 step 6 names all three operations and the demo step; Skill 4 gains *is the Writer logging at all?* and *is the DLQ full of 23514?*; Skill 5 gains the whole-window check; Skill 6 uses DL-151 as its worked example; Skill 8 gains the MDC rule. IMPLEMENTATION_RULES.md 1.4: the count, three quiz rows, the device-path tests. ADR-007 §1: the path-parameter application. DATA-SENSITIVITY-INVENTORY.md: the URL path as a seventh sink, because clsbox and uid now travel in request paths this project does not log or retain. ops/prometheus/README.md: the source label. 3. One precision, recorded as an ADR-006 amendment rather than left as a contradiction. DL-142's amendment says a new tenant requesting a clsbox with only pre-move rows "receives 404, indistinguishable from not-found (STA-35B)". DL-151 says an unknown or foreign clsbox on the device-scoped paths is an empty 200, never 404. Both are right in their own operation: 404 is the uid *lookup*'s answer, the empty page is the device *list*'s, and on a list a 404 would disclose that another tenant's gateway exists. The ADR-006 *Amendment — 21 September 2026* says so, adds two register rows (the device paths, DECIDED; tenant binding on the external door, OPEN — T-068), and gives the tenant != maintenant fixture rule its write-side sibling for T-068's test. CLAUDE.md §7's D16 row says both.

Why in one change. DL-125 recorded the same failure on 5 September — the tracker held the day's code and none of its decisions — and the reason is unchanged: a reader who finds the tracker and the documents disagreeing cannot tell which is stale, and the tracker's Next Up is what the second owner works from. A reconciliation split across days is a reconciliation that will be found half-done.

Rejected. *Recording T-068 as TODO with a Blocked-by note*, as T-034 is — T-034's block (D-12 P3) is a decision engineering could take; T-068's (Q8a-2) is a mechanism ZC has to name, and nothing can be written against a mechanism that does not exist. *Rebuilding Stats_App_Developer_Guide.pdf* — it lives outside the repository, was built on 18 Sep, and predates DL-147 onward; regenerating it is its own task and is noted in the tracker rather than done silently here.

Consequence. Every document that names a count now names the same one: 15 loss windows (12 silent), 86 fixture files, 105 assertions, 91 Java parity cases, 689 tests, 69 tasks, 34 traps. The next entry that adds a loss window, a fixture or a trap has seven places to update; the tracker's Notes row for this entry lists them.

Phase one

Foundation

Platform, module boundaries, capabilities, build gates and startup controls

1.1 Separate services and shared code

Heimdal → HTTP POST → stats-input → RabbitMQ → stats-writer → PostgreSQL
                                                                 ↑
Reader → Keycloak token → GET → stats-api ─────────────────────────┘

Input validates the envelope and publishes. It has no database responsibility. Writer validates payloads and is the only application with database write capability. API reads as a restricted database role and has no broker connection. This separation makes a fault or scaling requirement in one responsibility independent of the others.

The decision

Three applications with distinct capabilities

Decision. Keep Input, Writer and API as separate deployables, with shared code in a library.

Why. The ingestion path, persistence work and read traffic have different dependencies and scaling needs.

Effect. Input has no database responsibility; Writer owns writes; API has no broker connection.

DL-013 · DL-014 · DL-035

One shared library, stats-common, carries shared models and contract components without becoming another runtime service. stats-testsupport is a separate plain test support module. An earlier Java test fixtures arrangement was replaced because its Gradle variant selection was problematic. API tests do not import the broker exporting test support library: that would violate the API's no broker dependency rule.

Java 21 and Spring Boot 3.5.16 are pinned through the Gradle Kotlin DSL wrapper and a single version catalogue. Dependency versions managed by Spring Boot move together through its BOM. Jackson handles JSON; NetworkNT 2.0.4 evaluates Draft 2020 12 schemas with Jackson 2 compatibility; Joni supplies ECMA 262 regex semantics. PostgreSQL 17 is the only database. Flyway SQL migrations suit partition DDL and avoid a second migration language. MongoDB and pg_partman are not dependencies. These choices and their alternatives are recorded in DL 010–027.

The decision

Pin the platform in one place

Decision. Use Java 21, Spring Boot 3.5.16 and the Gradle wrapper with a central version catalogue.

Why. An approximate version is not reproducible and module specific pins drift.

Effect. A platform update moves the managed dependency set together; SQL migrations remain with Flyway.

DL-010 · DL-011 · DL-012 · DL-024

Local development can run infrastructure in Compose and applications through Gradle for debugging. The apps profile runs everything in containers. The bundle delivers three application images plus infrastructure and test images; it does not collapse the architecture into one container. A single archive is a delivery unit, not a single service.

1.2 Database identities and startup health

Writer connects with the database identity that owns migrations and partition maintenance. API uses stats_ro, which has SELECT access but no write, schema creation or temporary table capability. Compose removes database credentials from Input and write credentials from API. Readiness checks dependencies; liveness checks process health, so a temporary infrastructure failure does not masquerade as a dead process.

The decision

Make a forbidden write fail in PostgreSQL

Decision. Enforce API read access with database grants as well as the GET only HTTP contract.

Why. A coding convention cannot prevent a future accidental write.

Effect. Capability tests prove that the read role cannot mutate data; default grants also cover future Flyway tables.

DL-135

1.3 Build checks and logging conventions

CI runs the Gradle build with real PostgreSQL and RabbitMQ Testcontainers. The schema catalogue has one authoritative location; lint checks OpenAPI structure and parity separately checks schema equality. Source guards catch duplicate configuration keys, unsafe error logging, MDC key shadowing and prohibited dependency coupling. Structured JSON logs use scoped delivery identifiers, and full payload logging depends on DEBUG level.

The decision

Test the real contract and the real dependency

Decision. Use real database and broker containers, then check schema parity and assertion counts separately from syntax lint.

Why. A mock cannot prove a real grant, queue argument or database lock; a green test can conceal a shortened fixture set.

Effect. Build gates detect drift before a bundle reaches another machine.

DL-015 · DL-025 · DL-098 · DL-117 · DL-140

The foundation decisions

Expand a dated record for the complete rationale, alternatives, consequences and verification. These records belong to this phase. Later amendments remain visible in their historical context.

DL-010 — Java 21

Records, pattern matching, and virtual threads are all directly useful here — the envelope is a record, and the Input module is an I/O-bound HTTP front end. Java 21 is the current LTS, so the choice does not need revisiting inside V1. The toolchain pin matters as much as the version: a build that compiles differently on two machines is not a build.

13 Aug 2026 · RATIFIED · ADR-001

Decision. Java 21, pinned through a Gradle toolchain rather than inherited from whatever JDK is on PATH.

Why. Records, pattern matching, and virtual threads are all directly useful here — the envelope is a record, and the Input module is an I/O-bound HTTP front end. Java 21 is the current LTS, so the choice does not need revisiting inside V1. The toolchain pin matters as much as the version: a build that compiles differently on two machines is not a build.

Rejected. Kotlin — the team and the wider Datahub Core work are Java; a second language for one add-on is a maintenance tax with no offsetting gain here. Java 17 — the SRS implied it, but it buys nothing and expires sooner.

Consequence. CI, images, and developer machines all need JDK 21.

DL-011 — Spring Boot 3.5.16, pinned exactly, in one place

ZC answered "> 3.5.x", which is not a version — it is a range, and a range does not reproduce a build. G6 pinned an exact one. The single-entry rule is the more valuable half: bumping springBoot moves the whole dependency set as one tested, coherent unit, instead of a pile of independently drifting pins that nobody has ever tested together.

18 Aug 2026 · RATIFIED · Gate G6, ADR-001

Decision. springBoot = "3.5.16" as a single entry in gradle/libs.versions.toml. Every module imports the Boot BOM. No module build file declares a version of anything Boot manages.

Why. ZC answered "> 3.5.x", which is not a version — it is a range, and a range does not reproduce a build. G6 pinned an exact one. The single-entry rule is the more valuable half: bumping springBoot moves the whole dependency set as one tested, coherent unit, instead of a pile of independently drifting pins that nobody has ever tested together.

Rejected. Leaving it provisional pending Q1a — Q1a was answered the same day (there is no internal reference repo), so there was never anything to reconcile against and the pin is final. Per-module versions — that is how a multi-module build acquires two Jackson versions and a NoSuchMethodError at runtime.

Consequence. A future ZC platform standard is a one-line change. That is the whole reason for the single entry.

DL-012 — Gradle, Kotlin DSL, wrapper only, version catalog

Gradle was a given from the requirements. Kotlin DSL over Groovy for type-safe accessors and real IDE completion — a mistyped Groovy property fails at execution time, a mistyped Kotlin one fails at compile time. Wrapper only means no developer needs a matching Gradle installed, and CI runs the same Gradle every machine does.

13 Aug 2026 · RATIFIED · ADR-001

Decision. Gradle multi-module with the Kotlin DSL, the Gradle Wrapper as the only supported entry point, and gradle/libs.versions.toml as the single source of dependency versions.

Why. Gradle was a given from the requirements. Kotlin DSL over Groovy for type-safe accessors and real IDE completion — a mistyped Groovy property fails at execution time, a mistyped Kotlin one fails at compile time. Wrapper only means no developer needs a matching Gradle installed, and CI runs the same Gradle every machine does.

Rejected. Maven — not what the requirements specify. Groovy DSL — silently tolerates typos. A globally installed Gradle — reintroduces "works on my machine" at the build-tool layer.

Consequence. One Kotlin DSL quirk is already load-bearing and commented in the root build: the generated libs accessor resolves against the project whose scope it is evaluated in, so inside subprojects { } it must be captured from the root first (val catalog = libs).

DL-013 — Four Gradle modules, three Docker images

The three application modules have genuinely different scaling and failure profiles — Input is sized for a 10× synchronised burst, Writer for sustained drain, the API for read concurrency. Separate images let each scale and fail alone. The dependency rule is what keeps that true: one import from stats-api to stats-writer would put a database write path in the read-only service and quietly break the "only Module 2 writes" rule (STA-32).

13 Aug 2026 · ANSWERED (Q2) · ADR-001, settings.gradle.kts

Decision. stats-common (library), stats-input, stats-writer, stats-api (images). Each application module may depend on stats-common; never on another application module.

Why. The three application modules have genuinely different scaling and failure profiles — Input is sized for a 10× synchronised burst, Writer for sustained drain, the API for read concurrency. Separate images let each scale and fail alone. The dependency rule is what keeps that true: one import from stats-api to stats-writer would put a database write path in the read-only service and quietly break the "only Module 2 writes" rule (STA-32).

Rejected. One image with profiles — a poison payload crash-loops all three roles at once. A shared "core" module between application modules — that is the import that breaks the write-isolation rule; stats-common is the only shared node and it is deliberately narrow (DL-014).

Consequence. The Spring Boot plugin is declared at the root and applied only in the three application modules; stats-common is a plain java-library and produces no executable.

DL-014 — stats-common holds exactly five things

Each of the five is shared *by construction*, not by convenience. The topology constants are the sharpest case: two modules declaring the same queue with different arguments does not warn — RabbitMQ rejects the mismatched declaration with RESOURCE_LOCKED and the module fails to start. Declaring them once is the fix, not the tidy-up.

24 Aug 2026, extended 29 Aug 2026 · RATIFIED · CLAUDE.md §2, decision D15

Decision. The envelope model, the stat-type enum, the AMQP topology constants, RFC 9457 Problem Details with the error catalogue, and Draft 2020-12 schema validation. No module-specific application logic, ever.

Why. Each of the five is shared *by construction*, not by convenience. The topology constants are the sharpest case: two modules declaring the same queue with different arguments does not warn — RabbitMQ rejects the mismatched declaration with RESOURCE_LOCKED and the module fails to start. Declaring them once is the fix, not the tidy-up.

The validator was the contested addition. It belongs here because docs/schemas/ is normative for the whole project and the Java oracle must stay in parity with the Python CI gate — a shared capability, not Input's business logic.

Rejected. A general "common" or "util" module — this is exactly how a shared module becomes a dumping ground that every module must be rebuilt against. Duplicating the topology constants per module — the RESOURCE_LOCKED failure above.

Consequence. Adding anything to stats-common needs an argument that it is common to all three application modules. The build file names the five and says so.

DL-015 — CI is ./gradlew build plus Testcontainers on every push

With no reference project to inherit conventions from (Q1a), these had to be chosen rather than copied. The fixture gate is the specific one worth naming: docs/schemas/ is normative, and the fixtures are the only mechanism that keeps the Java validator and the Python validator agreeing about what a valid record is. A drift there is silent in both directions.

18 Aug 2026 · ENGINEERING · ADR-001, 2B-ANSWERS-18AUG2026.md §1

Decision. Every push runs the full build and the Testcontainers integration tests. The build fails on a failed schema-fixture assertion.

Why. With no reference project to inherit conventions from (Q1a), these had to be chosen rather than copied. The fixture gate is the specific one worth naming: docs/schemas/ is normative, and the fixtures are the only mechanism that keeps the Java validator and the Python validator agreeing about what a valid record is. A drift there is silent in both directions.

Rejected. Integration tests on a schedule or pre-merge only — the failures being guarded against (topology mismatch, retention boundaries, tenant leakage) are precisely the ones a unit test cannot see.

Consequence. CI needs a Docker daemon. Accepted; there is no way to test partitions, quorum queues, or ON CONFLICT semantics against a mock and learn anything true.

DL-020 — Jackson databind is a compile-time api dependency of stats-common

Two envelope decisions force it into the public contract. data is a JsonNode, because the D14 unknown-field policy requires unknown members inside data to be preserved — a typed DTO would silently drop them. And Timestamps carries Jackson converters so the wire keeps Unix seconds while Java sees Instant. A consumer of StatsEnvelope cannot use it without Jackson, so hiding it behind implementation would be a lie the compiler would eventually catch.

24 Aug 2026 · FROM CODE · stats-common/build.gradle.kts

Decision. stats-common exposes Jackson on its public API (api(libs.jackson.databind)), not merely as an implementation detail.

Why. Two envelope decisions force it into the public contract. data is a JsonNode, because the D14 unknown-field policy requires unknown members inside data to be preserved — a typed DTO would silently drop them. And Timestamps carries Jackson converters so the wire keeps Unix seconds while Java sees Instant. A consumer of StatsEnvelope cannot use it without Jackson, so hiding it behind implementation would be a lie the compiler would eventually catch.

Rejected. Map<String, Object> for data — loses ordering and node types and makes byte-identical republication harder. A generated typed DTO per stat type at the envelope level — breaks the additive-new-type rule (STA-14A).

Consequence. stats-common still has no Spring context, no broker client and no JDBC driver. That boundary is the one being protected; Jackson is not on the wrong side of it.

DL-021 — NetworkNT json-schema-validator for Draft 2020-12

24 Aug 2026 · ENGINEERING · libs.versions.toml, StatsSchemaValidator

Decision. com.networknt:json-schema-validator, kept private behind our own ValidationResult API.

Why this library. Jackson can parse and represent a JSON tree but cannot evaluate a JSON Schema — that is not something it does. Draft 2020-12 support is required because the schema catalogue uses it, and NetworkNT is the mature JVM implementation with it. It is wrapped rather than exposed so the validator can be replaced without touching every caller.

Rejected. Hand-written validation — five schemas with patternProperties, $defs, conditional requirements and the deliberate ts.converter rejection; hand-rolling this guarantees divergence from the normative files and from the Python gate. Bean Validation annotations alone — cannot express the schema catalogue, and would put the normative rules in two places.

Consequence. NetworkNT error messages are deliberately discarded — they can embed the offending value, and echoing attacker-controlled input into a 400 response is how a validator becomes a reflection gadget. Only JSON Pointers, machine-readable codes and reviewed SafeMessage constants cross the public boundary (STA-03A).

DL-022 — NetworkNT 2.x, not 3.x — pinned at 2.0.4

NetworkNT 3.x is built on Jackson 3. This is a Spring Boot 3 project and Spring Boot 3 manages Jackson 2. Taking 3.x would put two Jackson major versions on one classpath — the failure is a runtime NoSuchMethodError or NoClassDefFoundError, not a build error, so it would be found in a container rather than in CI.

24 Aug 2026 · ENGINEERING · libs.versions.toml

Decision. Pin jsonSchemaValidator = "2.0.4" and do not take the 3.x line.

Why. NetworkNT 3.x is built on Jackson 3. This is a Spring Boot 3 project and Spring Boot 3 manages Jackson 2. Taking 3.x would put two Jackson major versions on one classpath — the failure is a runtime NoSuchMethodError or NoClassDefFoundError, not a build error, so it would be found in a container rather than in CI.

Rejected. Latest-is-best — here it is a classpath conflict wearing the word "upgrade". Shading — a large hammer for a dependency that is doing fine.

Consequence. This pin is coupled to DL-011. When Spring Boot moves to Jackson 3, NetworkNT 3.x becomes the correct version and not before. Note that ordering here; a routine dependency bump in the wrong order breaks validation at runtime.

DL-023 — Joni, for ECMA-262 regex semantics

24 Aug 2026 · ENGINEERING · libs.versions.toml, StatsSchemaValidator

Decision. Add org.jruby.joni:joni and configure NetworkNT to use JoniRegularExpressionFactory.

Why this library. JSON Schema specifies ECMA-262 regular expressions. Java's java.util.regex is not ECMA-262 — it differs on anchors, character classes, and escapes. The Python CI gate validates the same fixtures with Python's engine. Leaving the Java validator on Java regex means two validators that agree on the fixture corpus today and disagree on the first pattern that exercises a difference — accepting in one and rejecting in the other, with no error anywhere. Joni is the ECMA-262-compatible engine NetworkNT integrates with, and this is the one dependency whose entire job is *making two implementations agree*.

Rejected. Java regex — silent cross-implementation divergence, which is the failure the fixture parity gate exists to prevent. Writing patterns in a "safe subset" both engines share — unwritable and unenforceable; nothing checks it.

Consequence. One extra dependency, and clsbox / counter patterns behave identically in validate-fixtures.py and StatsSchemaValidator. That parity is asserted by SchemaFixtureParityTest.

DL-024 — Flyway, not Liquibase

24 Aug 2026 · RATIFIED · ADR-003 D6

Decision. Flyway, with flyway-database-postgresql (mandatory from Flyway 10 onward for PostgreSQL support).

Why this library. The migrations here are partition DDL, a composite primary key with a specific column order, and index definitions — plain PostgreSQL, some of it not expressible in a database-agnostic abstraction at all (DETACH PARTITION CONCURRENTLY, pg_try_advisory_lock). Flyway runs plain .sql files, so a migration is reviewable as the SQL that will actually execute.

Rejected. Liquibase — its XML/YAML changelogs buy database portability this project explicitly does not want (PostgreSQL is the only V1 persistence technology), and they obscure exactly the DDL that most needs reading. It was provisional only pending Q1a; Q1a closed with no reference project mandating it. Hand-run SQL scripts — no version tracking, no repeatable environment.

Consequence. Migrations are owned by stats-writer and reviewed like code.

DL-025 — Testcontainers for PostgreSQL and RabbitMQ; no mocks for either

13 Aug 2026 · RATIFIED · ADR-001, IMPLEMENTATION_RULES.md §10

Decision. Integration tests run against real PostgreSQL and real RabbitMQ in containers. Retention and index tests must run against a real PostgreSQL — a mock is not acceptable evidence.

Why this library. Every behaviour that actually matters here is a real engine behaviour: ON CONFLICT (ts_device, uid) DO NOTHING returning zero affected rows, partition pruning, RESOURCE_LOCKED on a mismatched queue declaration, quorum-queue TTL dead-lettering, advisory locks. A mock returns whatever the test author believed — which is the belief under test.

Rejected. H2 or an embedded PostgreSQL — no real partitioning, different ON CONFLICT behaviour, so a green test proves nothing about production. A mocked RabbitTemplate — cannot exhibit a publisher confirm, a return callback, or a TTL bounce, which is to say it cannot test any of the reliability design.

Consequence. CI needs Docker (DL-015), and the test suite is slower. Both accepted.

DL-026 — Spring Boot Actuator for separate liveness and readiness probes

24 Aug 2026 · RATIFIED · Decision D13

Decision. Actuator, with management.endpoint.health.probes.enabled=true. Readiness probes only that module's real dependencies.

Why this library. It is in the platform already and gives liveness, readiness and Prometheus metrics from one starter. The important half is the *separation*: a single health endpoint that checks everything means a broker blip restarts the container instead of removing it from the load balancer for thirty seconds.

Rejected. A hand-written /health — reimplements what the platform ships, and always ends up as one combined check. One shared probe definition across modules — Input's readiness must not include the database (it has no database dependency at all, by rule 2), and the API's must not include the broker (it has no broker connection at all, ADR-002 §11).

Consequence. Each module's readiness set is written per module and is part of the review; a copied readiness block is a bug.

DL-027 — No MongoDB, and no pg_partman

MongoDB is a v1.0 document artefact — the decision went to PostgreSQL and is not deferred. It is listed here because the superseded .docx sources still say otherwise and a cached memory of them is a live risk.

24 & 29 Aug 2026 · RATIFIED · CLAUDE.md §3, ADR-003 D1

Decision. MongoDB is out of scope entirely. pg_partman is never a dependency; partition pre-creation is a scheduled task inside stats-writer behind the D3 advisory lock.

Why. MongoDB is a v1.0 document artefact — the decision went to PostgreSQL and is not deferred. It is listed here because the superseded .docx sources still say otherwise and a cached memory of them is a live risk.

pg_partman fails on its own terms: managed PostgreSQL frequently does not expose the extension, so building on it imports a platform risk for no gain — and the advisory lock is needed regardless, because D8 deploys N writer replicas per stat type and they would otherwise race. Given the lock exists, the extension adds a dependency and removes nothing.

Rejected. Making pg_partman conditional — a conditional dependency is two code paths, one of which is exercised only in the environment you did not test.

Consequence. Partition pre-creation is ours to write, test and monitor. If partitions are not pre-created, inserts fail — this is on the critical path, not a maintenance nicety.

DL-054 — docs/schemas/ is the single source; the build copies it, nothing is checked in twice

The schemas are normative and are validated by two independent implementations — validate-fixtures.py in CI and StatsSchemaValidator in Java. A checked-in second copy is a file that *can* drift, and a drift here means the two validators disagree about what a valid record is, silently, in opposite directions. Making the copy a build step makes drift impossible rather than unlikely.

24 Aug 2026 · FROM CODE · stats-common/build.gradle.kts

Decision. processResources copies docs/schemas/*.schema.json into stats-common's generated output, and the test source set reads docs/schemas/fixtures directly. No second copy is checked in.

Why. The schemas are normative and are validated by two independent implementations — validate-fixtures.py in CI and StatsSchemaValidator in Java. A checked-in second copy is a file that *can* drift, and a drift here means the two validators disagree about what a valid record is, silently, in opposite directions. Making the copy a build step makes drift impossible rather than unlikely.

Rejected. Checking schemas into src/main/resources — the drift above. Loading them from the filesystem at runtime — the container would need docs/ shipped in the image.

Consequence. Schemas are edited in docs/schemas/ only. The Gradle test task also passes stats.repositoryRoot so StatTypeFanOutTest can read the other modules' sources to prove no code special-cases an individual stat type (STA-22).

DL-064 — The Keycloak realm was built rather than waited for

Q8b was answered by building it. A proposal that runs is a configuration change away from correct if ZC disagrees; a proposal in a document is a meeting away from anything. It unblocked the whole dev stack.

18 Aug 2026 · PROPOSED (Q8b; awaiting Q8b-2) · ADR-006, docs/setup/keycloak-realm-statsapp.json

Decision. Realm statsapp; clients stats-api (confidential resource server, no flows), stats-input (service account), stats-dev-cli (public, dev only); roles stats.readOnly and stats.ingest; audience stats-api; tenant claim tenant, single-valued String. Committed as an importable realm.

Why. Q8b was answered by building it. A proposal that runs is a configuration change away from correct if ZC disagrees; a proposal in a document is a meeting away from anything. It unblocked the whole dev stack.

Two details are decisions in their own right. The read role is stats.readOnly — not the bare readOnly placeholder; four spellings were in circulation for a security control. And iss is compared as a string, never fetched, so one issuer value stays correct whether a module runs on the host or inside compose; containerised modules get jwk-set-uri pointed at the compose hostname, which also disables OIDC discovery — which would otherwise try to GET the issuer URL and fail.

Rejected. Waiting for ZC — indefinite block on a config change. Reusing a Datahub Core realm — none was specified; if one exists it replaces this by configuration.

Consequence. Values are provisional pending Q8b-2, but the shape is not. Rejecting a token minted for another audience is what stops a neighbouring service's token being replayed here.

DL-080 — Structured JSON to stdout; uid as correlation ID; full payloads only at DEBUG

stdout because the platform collects it; JSON because the alternative is a regex per log line at query time. uid as correlation ID lets one record be traced from the HTTP request through the queue to the row — which is the question actually asked during an incident.

18 Aug 2026 · ENGINEERING · decision D1, ADR-001 · AMENDED 4 Sep 2026 by DL-102

Decision. One JSON event per line to stdout, traceId and uid on every record, full payloads only at DEBUG, which is off in production, no tokens or secrets ever at any level.

> Amended 4 Sep 2026 (DL-102). This entry originally said *"no payloads on the > success path"*, which was true and incomplete: it left the failure path unstated and > two other documents then read it as permission. The control is the level, not the > path — DEVELOPMENT.md §7 is authoritative and always said so.

Why. stdout because the platform collects it; JSON because the alternative is a regex per log line at query time. uid as correlation ID lets one record be traced from the HTTP request through the queue to the row — which is the question actually asked during an incident.

No payloads on the success path is a volume decision *and* a data-handling one: at 115.2M records/day, logging payloads is both unaffordable and a copy of production data in the log store.

Rejected. A generated request ID — real, but does not survive the queue hop into the Writer. Logging payloads on success — cost and data exposure.

Consequence. Failure paths log enough context to act on — but that context is fields, not the record. Every failure path either returns a meaningful error, dead-letters, or logs at WARN/ERROR — never swallowed, and never with the payload attached above DEBUG.

DL-081 — Secrets come from injected environment variables only

Anything in application.properties is in the image and in Git history, which is not a place a secret can be removed from. Making the dev values *obviously* fake matters more than making them absent: a dev stack that needs real credentials to run is a dev stack people copy production credentials into.

18 Aug 2026 · ENGINEERING · ADR-001

Decision. Secrets are injected as environment variables by the platform — never in application.properties, images, or Git. The dev stack uses literal dev-only values that are obviously fake.

Why. Anything in application.properties is in the image and in Git history, which is not a place a secret can be removed from. Making the dev values *obviously* fake matters more than making them absent: a dev stack that needs real credentials to run is a dev stack people copy production credentials into.

Rejected. A secrets manager SDK — a dependency and a platform coupling for something env vars already do; the platform can back them with a manager transparently.

Consequence. application.properties uses ${VAR} placeholders. Where a value is genuinely undecided it is commented out, not guessed — an unanswered question must fail closed (absent) rather than fail open (a plausible-looking default).

DL-092 — Shared Testcontainers support lives in stats-common Java test fixtures

Persistence and broker tests need the same real services, image versions, and Spring Boot service-connection wiring. Test fixtures reuse this infrastructure without adding a sixth stats-common/src/main responsibility or allowing module copies to drift.

31 Aug 2026 · ENGINEERING · T-008, DL-014, DL-015, DL-025 · SUPERSEDED 3 Sep 2026 by DL-099

> Superseded. The placement changed to a plain stats-testsupport module; the > *reasoning* below — one shared definition, never on a production classpath, no > per-module copies to drift — is unchanged and is what DL-099 preserves. Only the > Gradle mechanism moved, because IDE Gradle importers do not resolve the > test-fixtures variant.

Decision. Shared PostgreSQL 17 and RabbitMQ 4 Testcontainers support lives in stats-common's java-test-fixtures source set. It is reusable build/test code, not production code.

Why. Persistence and broker tests need the same real services, image versions, and Spring Boot service-connection wiring. Test fixtures reuse this infrastructure without adding a sixth stats-common/src/main responsibility or allowing module copies to drift.

Rejected. A separate Gradle project — it would look like a fourth deployable module. Per-module support copies — image and lifecycle configuration would drift.

Consequence. Each support owns one static container per test JVM. This reuse is within a Gradle worker/JVM only; it is not Docker-level reuse across workers or separate JVMs, and .withReuse(true) remains prohibited.

DL-098 — The fixture gate asserts the assertion count, not just that they passed

The gate reported {checked} assertions checked and exited non-zero only on a mismatch, so deleting fixtures was invisible: checked simply dropped and CI stayed green. The fixture corpus is the oracle that caught four wrong payload schemas and every wrong envelope field name on 29 Aug 2026 — a gate that cannot notice its own oracle shrinking is not protecting it.

3 Sep 2026 · ENGINEERING · T-007, Q11a

Decision. docs/schemas/validate-fixtures.py fails when the number of assertions it ran differs from EXPECTED_ASSERTIONS (87), in addition to failing on any mismatch. Changing the number is a deliberate edit that belongs in the same commit as the fixture change.

Why. The gate reported {checked} assertions checked and exited non-zero only on a mismatch, so deleting fixtures was invisible: checked simply dropped and CI stayed green. The fixture corpus is the oracle that caught four wrong payload schemas and every wrong envelope field name on 29 Aug 2026 — a gate that cannot notice its own oracle shrinking is not protecting it.

Rejected. An environment-variable override — an escape hatch on an assertion whose whole value is that it cannot be skipped by accident. Counting fixture *files* — the Java parity test expands to 80 schema cases against the gate's 87 because $expect markers are not schema cases (ADR-003 D9); counting assertions keeps the two numbers explainable rather than coincidentally equal.

Consequence. Adding or removing a fixture is a two-line change. That is the intended friction.

DL-099 — Test infrastructure is a plain stats-testsupport module, not a test-fixtures variant

The test-fixtures variant is correct Gradle and builds cleanly — but the IDE could not resolve it. VS Code's Java support imports this repository through Buildship (org.eclipse.buildship.core.gradleclasspathcontainer, confirmed in the generated .classpath), and Buildship's Eclipse project model has no representation for the testFixtures source set. The result was The import com.zenner.datahub.stats.testsupport cannot be resolved in eleven files across stats-common and stats-writer — every Docker-backed integration test in the project, which is to say the tests covering tenant isolation, the ACK boundary, retention, and the loss windows. Those are the files a reviewer most needs to be able to navigate, and in the IDE they had no completion, no go-to-definition, and no refactoring.

3 Sep 2026 · ENGINEERING · T-008, ADR-001 · Supersedes the *placement* in DL-092

Decision. PostgresSupport and RabbitSupport move to a new stats-testsupport module (java-library, no Spring Boot plugin, no image). Consumers depend on it with testImplementation(project(":stats-testsupport")). stats-common no longer applies java-test-fixtures. Package names are unchanged, so no import anywhere moved.

Why. The test-fixtures variant is correct Gradle and builds cleanly — but the IDE could not resolve it. VS Code's Java support imports this repository through Buildship (org.eclipse.buildship.core.gradleclasspathcontainer, confirmed in the generated .classpath), and Buildship's Eclipse project model has no representation for the testFixtures source set. The result was The import com.zenner.datahub.stats.testsupport cannot be resolved in eleven files across stats-common and stats-writer — every Docker-backed integration test in the project, which is to say the tests covering tenant isolation, the ACK boundary, retention, and the loss windows. Those are the files a reviewer most needs to be able to navigate, and in the IDE they had no completion, no go-to-definition, and no refactoring.

A build layout that only the command line understands is a real defect, not a preference. ./gradlew build passing is necessary and not sufficient.

Rejected. Telling developers to upgrade or re-configure the extension — it makes a correct checkout depend on per-machine tooling state, and the next person hits it again. Configuring the eclipse plugin to splice the fixtures output into each consumer's classpath — carries the same fragility with more moving parts, and has to be repeated for every new consumer. Copying the support classes per module — the drift DL-092 correctly rejected.

Consequence. One more entry in settings.gradle.kts that is not a deployable and not a fourth application module; the module's build file says so at the top, and the dependency is testImplementation, so nothing it brings in can reach an application's runtime classpath. StatTypeFanOutTest scans it like any other module, so the no-stat-type-outside-StatType guard still covers everything. Q2 is untouched: still three Docker images.

DL-102 — Payload logging is gated by LEVEL, not by path; and D-12 gets its input document

Three documents disagreed, and two of the three read as blanket permission:

4 Sep 2026 · ENGINEERING · D-12, D1, DL-080, ADR-001, ADR-002 §12 · T-034

Decision. Two things.

1. DEVELOPMENT.md §7 is the authoritative logging rule and the other documents are reconciled to it. The rule is level-gated: *full payloads only at DEBUG, which is off in production; never at INFO or above on any path, success or failure; tokens and secrets never, at any level; payload logging stays configurable.* DL-080's title and decision are amended, IMPLEMENTATION_RULES.md §8 is rewritten, and D-12's *decided* half in CODE_START_CHECKLIST.md now states the level gate instead of the success path alone.

2. docs/decisions/DATA-SENSITIVITY-INVENTORY.md is written — every field in all six schemas, the six sinks it can reach, and what class of identifier it is. It is an input document, not a classification; the five questions in its §6 are still security and compliance's to answer.

Why. Three documents disagreed, and two of the three read as blanket permission:

Source Said
CODE_START_CHECKLIST.md D-12, DL-080 "no payloads on the success path" — true, but silent about failure
IMPLEMENTATION_RULES.md §8 "V1 permits payload logging to the Java logger/Docker logs" — no level, no path
DEVELOPMENT.md §7 "DEBUG — full payloads. Off in production"; "never … full payloads at INFO or above"

Only DEVELOPMENT.md stated a rule an implementer could follow. This is the same failure as the 401/403 contract defect: several documents agreeing on an ambiguity, which consistency then disguises. It mattered because a logged ltestats payload is an IMSI in the Docker log store, so an unqualified permission was quietly pre-deciding the masking half of D-12 by default.

What the inventory found that changes D-12's shape. D-12 names one sensitive field. ltestats carries three classes on the same record: subscriber (imsi), location (lac/cid, refined by the signal fields) and carrier (plmn/operator) — and every record of every type also carries clsbox, a stable device id. Masking IMSI alone leaves a per-device location trail keyed on clsbox, sampled every ten minutes for ninety days. Separately, uid, tenant and clsbox are on every log line on the success path by design at 115.2M records/day, under a retention policy that does not exist — the largest-volume item in D-12 and not what the row was about.

The deadline is T-034, not a date. ltestats is unbuilt, so no IMSI, cell id or IP exists in PostgreSQL, in a log or in a queue today; every item is still a free config choice. After T-034, ADR-002 §12's *no TTL, ever* — correct, and what stops silent loss — is exactly what makes an unmasked identifier permanent in the DLQ, and the answer then costs a migration, an API contract change, and a purge that ADR-002 §12 forbids.

Rejected. *Reconciling toward "no payloads, ever"* — stricter than the business decision recorded in DEVELOPMENT.md §7 (D2, NFR-03), and it would delete the one tool that makes a malformed record diagnosable. *Filing the contradiction as a question for security* — it is a documentation defect between three engineering documents; security cannot resolve which of our own files is right. *Deciding the masking policy here* — the irreversible half (P4, P5 in the inventory) genuinely needs a signature.

Consequence. Two exposure paths remain open and are named in the inventory §5 rather than fixed here: a PostgreSQL DETAIL: line can carry payload values into an ERROR log, and data_extra accepts identifiers no field-level rule anticipates. D-12 still has no T-nnn task, so nothing in the tracker will surface it — the Q14a defect, repeated.

DL-103 — Readiness probes each module's real dependencies, because Boot's default fails open

D-10's *decided* half has claimed since 29 August that readiness "probes only each module's real dependencies" (D13). It did not. No module set management.endpoint.health.group.readiness.include, so Boot's default readiness group is readinessState alone — the endpoint reported UP with PostgreSQL and RabbitMQ both down. All three docker/docker-compose.yml health-checks target exactly that URL.

4 Sep 2026 · ENGINEERING · D13, D-10, T-006, ADR-002 §11, STA-32 · DL-100

Decision. All three modules configure both probe groups explicitly:

Module Readiness Why
stats-input readinessState, rabbit The broker and nothing else. Input holds no database dependency at all (rule 2, STA-32), so a db indicator here would be evidence the rule had been broken, not a more thorough probe
stats-writer readinessState, db, rabbit It consumes from one and commits to the other and cannot work with either gone
stats-api readinessState, db Never rabbit — Q3a and ADR-002 §11 give it no broker connection, so the absence is part of that decision

Liveness is written out as livenessState in all three even though that is already Boot's default, so the pair is visible and neither silently drifts.

Why. D-10's *decided* half has claimed since 29 August that readiness "probes only each module's real dependencies" (D13). It did not. No module set management.endpoint.health.group.readiness.include, so Boot's default readiness group is readinessState alone — the endpoint reported UP with PostgreSQL and RabbitMQ both down. All three docker/docker-compose.yml health-checks target exactly that URL.

It fails open, which is the direction that costs something: an orchestrator routes traffic to a Writer that cannot write, and a rolling deploy replaces healthy replicas with ones reporting ready before they can do any work. Combined with DL-100 — where stats-writer had no servlet container at all, so its healthcheck could never even be answered — the Writer's probe went from *unanswerable* to *answering UP unconditionally* without ever being right.

Rejected. *Putting dependencies in the liveness group* — liveness answers "should this process be restarted", and a database blip would then kill every replica while each replacement finds the same database still down. That is a restart loop that cannot fix itself. *A Keycloak indicator for stats-api* — Boot ships none for an OAuth2 issuer and the resource server itself lands with T-038; the probe says what it actually checks rather than implying one it does not. *Enabling show-details in production* — an unauthenticated probe should not enumerate dependencies; the tests set show-components themselves, which changes rendering and never membership.

Consequence. T-006 moves from PARTIAL to substantially closed: the groups are per-module and tested. What remains under it is the Keycloak probe (with T-038) and the stats-input structured-log field set, which is a different half of that task. ManagementSurfaceTest asserts the Writer's readiness names db and rabbit and that liveness names neither; ReadinessGroupTest in Input and the API assert the shipped configuration, since the defect being guarded is a configuration one and deleting a single line reintroduces it.

DL-113 — The version guard is written before the parser that would misuse it

4 Sep 2026 · ENGINEERING · trap 20, DL-105 rule 1, ADR-007 §4 (amended) · T-063

Decision. VersionIsNotADiscriminatorTest scans every Java main source and fails if any file outside a justified whitelist names the bare identifier version. Three entries are whitelisted today, each with its reason: StatsEnvelope declares the component, StatsSchemaValidator reads the *schema file's* own version — a different concept that happens to share the name — and LorastatsBatchWriter stores it verbatim as record_version, whose only conditional is a SMALLINT width check.

Why now, before T-063 rather than as part of it. DL-105 made this trap more dangerous, not less. Until 4 September there was no dialect-selection code at all, so nothing *could* branch on version. T-063 introduces structural dialect selection, and version will sit immediately beside it looking exactly like the discriminator it is not — all three production dialects carry version: 1. A guard written after the parser gets written to match whatever was built; written first, it has to be argued with, and the argument is the whole value.

Why a whitelist of files rather than a rule about branches. A test cannot prove an expression is not a discriminator without understanding the code. This detects a *shape*, like StatTypeFanOutTest before it: naming the identifier at all outside the passthrough mapping requires a whitelist entry with a written reason, which converts "someone quietly read version" into a reviewed decision. The regex is deliberately narrow — \bversion\b misses conversion, record_version and schemaVersion — so it does not fire on unrelated names.

Mutation-checked. Removing the StatsSchemaValidator exemption makes the test fail and name the file; restoring it passes. A guard that cannot fail is not a guard, which is the same standard trap 17's DLQ-TTL assertion is held to.

The failure it prevents is silent. Branching on version yields a parser that works perfectly on whichever dialect its author tested and mis-parses the other two into the same typed columns. The rows are *wrong*, not absent, and nothing fails — which is why this is a guard and not a code review note.

Consequence. Scanning moved into MainSourceScan, shared with StatTypeFanOutTest, so a module added to MODULES is covered by both guards at once. Trap 20's second half — the two dialect fixtures moving from invalid/ to valid/ — still belongs to T-063.

DL-117 — The API contract is linted in CI, because a YAML parse cannot see a broken contract

5 Sep 2026 · ENGINEERING · DL-070…DL-073, DL-100, DL-116, ADR-006, STA-38A · T-038, T-039–T-046

Decision. redocly lint runs on every push and pull request, pinned at 2.51.2, before the Gradle build. Its rule set is recommended with exactly two suppressions, each recorded with its reason in the new root redocly.yaml. Seven defects it found in docs/contracts/openapi.yaml are fixed in the same change.

The defect class. Five data-schema properties carried an unquoted description containing a comma inside a YAML flow mapping:

plmn: { type: string, pattern: '^[0-9]{5,6}$', description: Public land mobile network code, as a string. }

The comma closes the value and as a string. is parsed as a sibling key of the schema object. LtestatsData.plmn, .lac, .cid, SysstatsData.memfree and OmsstatsData.timespan each gained one or two nonsense keywords. Two further properties — SysstatsData.uptime and .idletime — were the same construct without a comma, so they parsed correctly today and would have broken the moment anyone edited the sentence; all seven are quoted, not just the five that failed.

Why a linter and not review. yaml.safe_load() accepts every one of these files without complaint, and so does a human read — the line looks like prose because it *is* prose. Only a validator that knows what an OpenAPI schema object may contain rejects it. This is the same shape as the trap in CLAUDE.md §6 about consistency across documents not being evidence of correctness: the file was internally consistent and wrong.

Why the two suppressions are decisions rather than noise. info-license-strict wants an SPDX identifier or URL on a licence that is Internal / Confidential — there is no such identifier, and satisfying the rule would mean asserting a licence that does not exist. no-server-example.com objects to http://localhost:8083/v1, which is deliberately present so a reader can exercise the API against docker/docker-compose.yml; the templated ingress leads the list. Both are turned off in config with the reasoning inline, so the gate exits zero on a correct contract. A gate that always emits warnings is a gate that is never read — DL-100.
